[28.08.2026 14:05] antonio110582._72773
I was thinking that, in the future, it could be really cool to have a simple platform where you could build your own custom OT firmware from a common core.

For example, you could start with the original Octatrack OS, or ideally with the 256 Kits OS that has already been developed instead of the 4 Parts system, and then simply choose which DSP effects you want: one delay, one reverb, a different filter, Lo-Fi, etc.

The idea would be to have a small “firmware builder” that takes the common OS core and lets you select the effects you want, then generates the firmware.

The important thing would be that the core functionality stays exactly the same. If you load the same project/song with another custom OS, everything should behave exactly the same, the only thing that changes is the DSP of the effects you selected.

So instead of having lots of completely different OS versions, you would basically have one common core and interchangeable effects.

Just an idea for the future, but I think it could be a really powerful and relatively clean way of bringing all these different developments together. I have an OT MKI and I’d be very happy to test this kind of thing.

{Reactions}
👍 (6) 🔥

[28.08.2026 14:11] bryant12345
I definitely like the idea of being able to pick/choose features. There’s some coordination that becomes a challenge for extensions. If I write something in place of the spring reverb, there’s now a conflict if users want both.

[28.08.2026 16:08] yldopactyly
I would really like to ask, and I know I ought to DYOR as well as RTFM but what is the hullabaloo about the Octatrack's memory being """"maxed out""""
At the end of the day are we stuck with a certain mostly packed memory space, and when we add new stuff we basically need to overwrite other things, basically switching out functional components?


[28.08.2026 16:09] yldopactyly
How feasible is it, or how feasible does it appear to be, to actually build on (or on top of)  the platform? Or are we rather doing what you could call edits?


[28.08.2026 16:24] bryant12345
I think each payload of the DSP firmware has 8192 words max. The payload with the least available words is A. I think that has 494 words free. Effects seem to range from roughly 300 to 1000 words.


[28.08.2026 16:26] bryant12345
The stuff I’ve focused on just edits words to change behavior because I didn’t want to eat up valuable free space.


[28.08.2026 16:30] bryant12345
But this is just on the DSP side. Folks doing work on the non-DSP parts might have more options. I don’t know the constraints there.


[28.08.2026 16:43] bamshanks
It’s pretty squished. They did an impressive job with what they had.

For me the value is being able to sacrifice things you don’t use and use those cycles and memory on things you do,

Give me the source code then that’s a different story. But can’t see that happening.


[28.08.2026 16:53] bryant12345
And I might be completely wrong on some of these numbers. I’ve verified a handful, but don’t know that 8192 with certainty.


[28.08.2026 16:54] bamshanks
Yeah I’ve got a burn test tool where I turn the knob until it starts squealing. Confirms around that number


[28.08.2026 16:55] bamshanks
But they have lots of little bits and pieces all over the place so you can never be sure


[28.08.2026 16:55] hallo_31886
Joined the server.

[30.08.2026 20:52] bukkakeborealis
Bricked it a few times already but always managed to access the OS upgrade menu at start up to flash a safe sysex firmware via midi


[30.08.2026 20:54] dvdmasr
glad to know! you access that when holding FUNC when turning on the OT right?


[30.08.2026 20:56] bukkakeborealis
Yes. And that’s only necessary if the brick happens at startup or before reaching the upgrade in the setup menu


[30.08.2026 20:57] dvdmasr
cool! i imagine its no issue reverting back to original firmware also?


[30.08.2026 20:59] bukkakeborealis
Yes. Just need to flash 1.40c and everything is fine. But the current project can be corrupted. Stuck sequencer happened to me. So I just created a new project


[30.08.2026 21:02] bukkakeborealis
I am not sure if there are chances to brick and then not be able access the startup menu. I don’t think so, but that would be catastrophic 🫣

[01.09.2026 23:00] rbl0k_28323
Claude mentioned 2 bugs in  scripts/analyze.sh:
missing "|| true" after "strings -n 6 "$f" | sort -u | head -40 | tee "$OUT/${base}.strings.txt" >/dev/null“

The `elektron-firmware-tool` call in the script only generates the report; 
it does not perform the actual extraction to `out/raw/` —it would need to be supplemented with a second call using 
`-d 3 -o out/raw` so that `make recon` actually delivers what its own comment promises.


[01.09.2026 23:05] bamshanks
Good finds! Patched now https://github.com/sambanks/octabam/pull/51

[03.09.2026 21:53] zackyoti_98392
Yo Team! I’m away from my OT for another week and a half, so can’t do proper hardware testing right now. But I’ve continued to build and do emulation testing and I’m just too excited not to share what I’ll be shipping when I get back. 

Y’all know I finished the midi plays free/direct bugfix, and was working on mutes+fx tails. So now the new mute mode has 4 options: OT original cut, OT original cut + fx tails, OT original  cut + fx tails in a trig mute style, and DT trig mute style (fx tails + sample follows its amp envelope after muting). Mutes as a result of soloing react according to whatever option is chosen.

That’s cool, but the other killer stuff includes new sidechain compression (pick any track as the key track, new bipolar filter before key track hits target, works even when key track is muted); Direct Jump à la Analog Rytm; and a nice QOL feature that deletes fully unlocked trigless locks from the grid after erase, to clean up visual noise (this one’s just scoped, but shouldn’t take too long to build. The rest of the features are near final. I’m sure the sidechain will need a little by-ear finessing).

You guys rock! Can’t wait to make the Kyoti firmware stuff  available in the coming weeks!

{Reactions}

[09.09.2026 11:02] zackyoti_98392
Just finished a new Reload feature tonight for OT. You know how once you change your pattern, unless you copied it to a new position prior, you can never “get back” to the original pattern (unless you reload the project i.e. stop the sequencer)? The newer boxes solved this with the Reload From Proj menu, and I always really liked that. So I added it to the OT, and it’s accessible from the front panel, not buried in a menu. Reload ALL PARTS, SEQUENCER DATA (trigs), or WHOLE PATTERN (parts and trigs). Now I want it for individual tracks too!

{Reactions}
👍 (5) 💯 (2)

FX2 Synth playing polyphonically with different chord sets on its own chromatic keyboard.

For what it's worth, whether my mods are included or not, in my dev setup I have a menu where I group all my experimental functions: It's accessible almost everywhere using a **double-tap on '*Function*'**, and the last function used stays highlighted when reopened (e.g., *fill sample pool, shuffle pool, create random scenes, rand fx, rand lfo, etc*).

[10.09.2026 02:38] emuyia
thought i should share this lead on more RAM acquisition. I was exploring ways to store kits without using the flex pool. one way requires repurposing part of the RAM reserved for the FAT, but it's quite invasive. the FAT takes up 8 MB regardless of CF card size, leaving some of the buffer that can be reserved for us, but 64 GB partitions at 32 KiB cluster size take up almost all the 8 MB, so 64 GB cards can't work. it's theoretically possible to have the octatrack support 64 KiB cluster sizes which is a path to allow 64 GB cards, but is another very invasive path to go down. regardless, doing this alone seems to require patching other areas too - like USB storage and doing on-device card formatting (although restoring those would be probably doable). I did create a test build to see if the kit data was happy, and it seemed so in initial testing. I guess the best outcome would be investigating the feasibility of 64 KiB cluster support, otherwise this path has the 64 GB compromise


[10.09.2026 02:46] freak_trash.

[10.09.2026 03:45] bryant12345
Silly question (treat me like i know nothing) … could you partition a 64 GB card into two 32 GB regions so you’d have 4 MB of RAM free on either, then do some magic to point to the appropriate partition when booting?


[10.09.2026 03:47] bryant12345
Honestly… if a person is willing to do that, they can just use two 32 GB cards.


[10.09.2026 04:31] emuyia
that's a creative idea. it got me thinking of similar partition related stuff. I think it could be another solid lead. keeping both partitions FATs in RAM would take roughly the full 8 MB buffer to read them both, in theory. but there's a 8 MB buffer separate from FAT that remembers cluster chains of loaded static samples. with a custom reader that tracks which partition each sample belongs to, that could potentially give playback access to both partitions without needing the full FAT. but browsing, loading and saving across the partitions would still need figuring out in this, and that might turn out comparably invasive to introducing 64 KiB cluster sizes as the original alternate solution


[10.09.2026 04:32] bamshanks
I imagine you would have to get deep in the internals to do a separate mount


[10.09.2026 04:37] emuyia
yeah. making both available simultaneously would probably mean untangling the filesystem's assumptions about having one mounted volume. the cluster maps might help with playback but they don’t solve mounting and file management stuff


[10.09.2026 04:38] bamshanks
Yeah and I don’t know much about these systems but I imagine a soft (or hard) link isn’t a thing?

[11.09.2026 06:30] avlantis
As someone who has been through the cycle (a couple times actually, due to financial needs to sell off at different stages), and generally bought and sold waaay too much over the last 20 years:

If you really love something, and it hasn't been replaced with a direct 1:1 upgrade - buuut it doesn't fit current setup/needs... 
Keep it, even if it's in a box tucked away somewhere. There are about 5-6 things I've sold for new/different purchases or need, and just miss because they were so good at being themselves.

In terms of deciding where to go next.. One thing I've learnt through the cycles is it doesn't matter how smart I am with features, routing, planning.. Things I learnt after the 'too much' realisation are:

- *Never* buy more than one piece of the new rig at a time. Firstly.. There will always be unknown gotchas about everything. Very easy for one weird MIDI response or something to break the whole setup planned. And if you've already invested in a whole concept, you will feel pressure to make do/workaround, or might have to sell off multiple things.

Besides gotchas, if you're like me - Don't connect something to the rig until you feel like you've mastered it alone. I would get caught up in the high level ideas of interaction/interconnection/overarching sequence/control, but unless you're using an incredibly simple box (eg an effect pedal), you don't know how you're going to mesh with it until you master it alone. Everything has a character, a particular 'soul' to how you play it, and it's not really obvious just from feature sets, videos, and reading the manual. It's the design philosophy, quirks, bugs, workarounds, shortcuts, the character of the sounds, the sweet spots, etc.

{Reactions}
❤️

[11.09.2026 06:54] avlantis
I think if you're building a concise 'rig' or 'setup'.. particularly if you're a live jammer.. always remember - you only have two hands and can probably only focus on one thing at a time (regarding complex boxes).

For me I have broken up my workflow as well to stop me getting bogged down in complexity and distracted.
- notes for ideas. so many I've forgotten/moved on without doing justice previously!
- sample/resource searching, collection, editing, managing. Maybe put presets here too. Honestly I feel like a big display is still the best way to do this, not to mention pc interface for web search/etc. I keep a big archive of everything, but I also build "packs" of ideas that might be good together. Usually based on the ideas in notes.
- composition - melodies, patterns, clips, chords, etc. Still usually just on PC using 'generic' sounds, unless it's a midi sequencing/modulating idea for specific hardware. Might start using Move for this.
 - song/production - putting stuff together in a project set that works.
-noodling/learning/configuring/experimenting for hardware, connecting things up. I do this when I'm not really focused or inspired. It helps to get the troubleshooting, unknowns, frustrations out of the way while you're not trying to create.
- serious live jam dev - try to avoid this until I have a full song idea ready. I do a lot of unserious live jamming attempts in above stage. Honestly hardly ever gotten to this stage, as I'm always off on tangents. Or tried to do it without having everything in a pack, and got sidetracked switching between transfer devices, looking for ideas that fit, etc..

I'm not good at sticking to it, but that's exactly why I came up with it and still trying lol.


[11.09.2026 06:56] avlantis
Oh and I'd also recommend collecting things that are just considered 'goated'. I have sold a fair few things over the years that have exploded in value down the track. I always used the 'utility' or 'better stuff' argument to get rid of it.. But wish I hadn't now!!

I think the main underlying line is just like you said 
- complexity is a burden
that +
- learn a piece of gear individually until you understand it's design/'soul'/character

{Reactions}
❤️

[13.09.2026 14:55] bamshanks
Yeah, the repo bills itself as an emulator but it has an assembler and disassembler in the tree too. We wrapped its assembler as our build's assembler, use its disassembler on the stock payloads, and run the emulator under our test harness.


[13.09.2026 14:55] bamshanks
- Assembler: https://github.com/dsp56300/dsp56300/blob/c051afad31612c2d2c7a81a7ab23e1c5ac9e61af/source/dsp56kEmu/assembler.cpp
- Disassembler core: https://github.com/dsp56300/dsp56300/blob/c051afad31612c2d2c7a81a7ab23e1c5ac9e61af/source/dsp56kEmu/disasm.cpp
- Disassembler CLI: https://github.com/dsp56300/dsp56300/blob/c051afad31612c2d2c7a81a7ab23e1c5ac9e61af/source/disassemble/disassemble.cpp


[13.09.2026 14:56] bamshanks
- The assembler wrapper: https://github.com/sambanks/octabam/blob/main/tools/harness/dsp_host/dsp_asm.cpp. It includes upstream's assembler.h and feeds it one instruction at a time, adding labels, org and the file handling upstream's class lacks.
- Where the build calls it: https://github.com/sambanks/octabam/blob/main/tools/build/build_bus.py
- Where the disassembler is called on the stock payloads: https://github.com/sambanks/octabam/blob/main/tools/build/dsp_disasm_all.py

[13.09.2026 16:01] buma2855
Octalab update: 
I worked on the « trig generation » side of Octalab yesterday.  I know there were several ideas floating around, like an euclidean sequencer, etc. 

I ended up implementing **MI GRIDS**. I came up with a pretty neat UX for changing page generation types (I call them "Grid Pages") in *GRID REC *mode , which I'll keep for another project. However, I'm dropping the GRIDS module. I played with the module a little bit, with a temporary UI, but the workflow remains complex and I really don't feel it brings anything interesting to the Octatrack.
 
Now I'm working on a "Grooves" system. This will be more interesting since it acts as an interface for the Octatrack's native functions (Micro Timing, Swing, etc.).

There have been other developments in Octalab as well, notably some quick randomization functions and a new shortcut for the MK1: 
*Trig + Bank in GRID REC mode to open the audio editor * directly on the Sample Lock of this trig or the slot assigned to the track.   
That's it for the update! 

WIP -> « octagrooves » unless someone is already working on something similar.

_______

For the grooves source, I have two options:

Option 1: https://magenta.withgoogle.com/datasets/groove 

Option 2: Using *Ableton Grooves .agr* as a source. Not the factory ones because the licence won't match but Ableton gives the possibility to extract a groove from MIDI or audio. So the user will be able to load grooves from the CF card. 

I think I'll go with option 2 + a couple of default grooves from Magenta.

{Embed}
https://magenta.withgoogle.com/datasets/groove
Groove MIDI Dataset
The Groove MIDI Dataset (GMD) is composed of 13.6 hours of aligned MIDI and (synthesized) audio of human-performed, tempo-aligned expressive drumming. The da...
https://images-ext-1.discordapp.net/external/SzY8gFnSB05W4-rxWAdYxulcsaLOM8vhmJuSHWBFU8s/https/magenta.withgoogle.com/assets/magenta-logo-card.jpg

[13.09.2026 20:59] bryant12345

[13.09.2026 21:17] buma2855
Here's where I'm at with Grooves. It works pretty well and it's super fun: you pick a groove per track (from files on the CF card, converted from Ableton Live .agr grooves, so you can use your own), and it's printed onto your trigs as micro timing and volume p-locks, with a global amount to push or calm the whole pattern. It still needs a lot of testing before any release.

@autopoiesis As I mentioned earlier, Grids is on hold for now. Sorry for getting your hopes up! 😔 

I might come back to it if I find a better way to fit it into the Octatrack.
As you know, the euro module has three outputs (BD, SN, HH) plus accents. On the Octatrack, I tried a system of "lanes", where each track picks which Grids output it plays. It works, but in testing the workflow felt a bit clunky. 

Unfortunately, the Octatrack doesn't really have a global page where you could host modulation or trig sources and play them in real time (...yet) hehe 🙂 

So Grids is parked, not dead. I'll get back to it if I find an integration that fits the Octatrack better than what I first designed. Ideas welcome!

More details about the project and screenshots here (no code yet, wip!): https://github.com/nordseele/octalab

[13.09.2026 22:03] bryant12345
Figured it out. I’d borrowed code that referenced tables in Payload A that didn’t map for Payload B. What that meant is that tracks 1-4 were using the wrong values since they are Payload B. Tracks 5-8 use Payload A and were fine. 

That was a slog to chase down. 

All my emulator tests were fine since that uses Payload A. But I’d flash the OT and Tracks 1 and 2 would misbehave.


[13.09.2026 22:13] bryant12345
Not sure if others will be as slow to figure this one out as I was!

[14.09.2026 20:38] bukkakeborealis
What is that bug ?


[14.09.2026 20:55] sezare56_40656
@BUKKAKEBOREALIS 
Buggy behaviors with REC SETUP and part changes.
The last tweaked value (RLEN for instance) can be maintained after a part change, without updating the new part value.

Ex :
Pattern 1 Part 1, SRC = T1
Pattern 2 Part 2, SRC = T2

If you tweak pattern 1 SRC to T8, then change to pattern 2, pattern 2 will record T8 instead of T2.

That’s why it can work after a power cycle. Maybe SAVE/RELOAD parts, bank or project works too.


[14.09.2026 21:03] bukkakeborealis
Ok so the UI is correct but after pattern change the last rec setup tweak is frozen behind? Just on rec setup?


[14.09.2026 21:10] buma2855
Just a quick update about grooves in Octalab: there's now a *groove pool* page per bank, with 8 groove slots, each with its own settings, plus a global amount. 

Each track picks a slot and takes its own share of the slot's timing and velocity. Change a slot, and every track on it follows. 

For now the pool lives in memory until power-off (the printed groove stays in the pattern); next step is a groove map file saved with the project. More in the README. 

Sorry for flooding the general channel, and for making the OT a bit more complicated with new shortcuts and pages...

{Reactions}
❤️

[14.09.2026 21:28] sezare56_40656

[14.09.2026 21:35] buma2855
Thanks! 
Yes, I'd love a randomize humanize too, it is planned to give it a try.  Ideally grooves should be randomisable, at the track level maybe, just like in Ableton Live. Right now everything is printed onto the trigs (micro timing and volume locks), so it's fixed once applied. "We're" (Claude and I lol) looking into how far we can hook into the generator that drives the OT's random trigs (trig probability) to get variation on every loop. We also try to stay away from the sequencer's playback engine itself: everything octalab does goes through data the OT already knows how to play, so timing stays rock solid. 

Building a groove system from scratch on the OT isn't easy, I admit 🙂  I was stuck on the UX for a while, but I think the concept of Groove pool and groove slots stays in the OT spirit: slots like the sample slots, one gesture, almost no extra layer to learn.

I'm all ears if anybody wants to chime in with workflow ideas, UI improvements etc. That's also why I'm posting here.


[14.09.2026 21:40] bryant12345
Awesome!


[14.09.2026 21:55] machinista__21901
Euclidean sequencer present in all newer Elektrons is actually pretty cool and seems not too complex - do you think having something like that on OT is feasible?


[14.09.2026 22:16] buma2855
Yes, I think it is feasible. But I don't know yet how it's done on the recent machines, if it's a generative layer on top of regular trigs or something else.

I don't have a Digitakt and I've only skimmed through the manual. 

I will look into it more closely. Trig generation is one third of my project. Still searching the best possible workflow for the OT. 

I was actually close yesterday when I implemented Grids on the OT, but ended up removing it because I didn't like the workflow. 

The alternative to a live generator (Euclidean or Grids or anything else) is a generator that "prints" the trigs, I mean in a destructive way with some kind of undo or pattern state recall, that's the way I implemented Grids and the way I do with Grooves etc.

I haven't given up on finding the right workflow for generators on OT yet I didn't know Euclidean was implemented on recent machines, I'll try to mimic that.


[14.09.2026 22:27] machinista__21901
In short this Euclidean on ST/DT/A4/.. is a "parallel" non-destructive layer - you can switch between regular Grid mode and Euclidean. You can also "print" the Euclidean into Grid in a destructive way, and then tweak further with p-locks etc


[14.09.2026 22:36] buma2855
Thanks, parallel and non destructive, yes I think its possible with this system of "saving the state" of track before entering Generative mode, and printing the new generated trigs if we're happy with that. 

That's what I had on my machine yesterday. The issue  when implementing new gestures and functions on the OT will always be the UX because all the buttons and combos are already assigned, and long time OT users are really familiar with all these shortcuts. Anyway, I think I've found some new ones 🙂 Generative stuff could live in what I call the Grid Rec Pages (arrow up and down in Grid rec mode)

I will look into Euclidean Sequencer for OT soon. 

I think the way Grids worked has been removed from the readme, but there might be some info left on my github repo nordseele/octalab

Edit: I've read the manual. I think nothing prevents an implementation on the OT. I'll probably try tomorrow !

[14.09.2026 22:54] bamshanks
Maybe in the Octabam channel instead of general if you have questions re octabam specifically. 

Please include full details on what you were doing, what step you were up to, what os etc. and if possible attempt to run it though your ai first and troubleshoot a bit.

Generally good etiquette to attach big error messages as text filesrather than dumping an error in the chat.

This is a discord is for hacking and sharing notes and code. People will try and help where they can but it’s not intended as a support  forum for end users. 

I’m sure people will package this stuff up nicely in future and provide support, but thats not the story here.

Octatrack has graduated from a dubious investment in unique but esoteric and depreciating hardware to a quasi-open platform, with a deluge of new tooling and mods coming out, custom firmware... it is a brave new age


[15.09.2026 20:00] yldopactyly

[15.09.2026 20:02] yldopactyly
The thing about OT is its functionality is simply not able to be approximated by any software solution. At the point you are plugging a crossfader into your $$$$ PC into a bespoke octatrack-like software with no tangible sequencer, you may as well just have an octatrack. one of the few electronic musical instruments that actually justifies its cost and the sand and ore it's built out of,  in purely economic terms.

[16.09.2026 14:39] buma2855
It's just crazy how many RE projects we're seeing these days. Some brands are going to be pissed...

Personally, apart from the Octatrack / Octalab project, I'm "working" on the Prophet 12 and the Nord Lead 4 in parallel. The Nord Lead is just for fun, I wanted to see how heavily Nord had encrypted its firmware. 

But the Prophet 12, I'm doing it to add MPE support (a long-requested feature, but the gear is discontinued, Dave Smith passed away, etc.). Everything was decompiled using Ghidra, well everything except for the Sharc part of course, since it can't handle it, but I don't really care for the DSP part of the synth. 

The only issue is that I ran into a brick wall because the synth apparently doesn't have a bootloader mode... sad so if I mess up, I brick a 3,500 euros synth... Right now I'm hesitating to invest in a PICkit so the agent can pull the processor contents directly. 

Crazy time...

___ 

If I hadn't stumbled upon this Octatrack RE project, I was about to sell it... that would've been a shame. It's always been a complicated relationship with the OT, but I gotta admit, now that I'm adding my own features, I'm loving it more and more.

[17.09.2026 01:50] anglingar
`We're using m-dwyer's digikit, an emulator for the ColdFire main CPU built on Unicorn, running Digitone II 1.11. We also use Digitakt II 1.15C as the better-supported reference. Where it stands for us:

- It boots and draws the real UI. The intro and the OS render to the 128×64 panel, and we can press buttons and capture screens. We save snapshots of the boot at 60M, 380M and 400M instructions, so runs start in seconds.
- It tests modified firmware before it touches hardware. We apply a patched image over a stock snapshot, or cold-boot it, and film the boot screen frame by frame. That's how we worked out the intro: it's a tunnel effect over a static logo bitmap. Custom boot animations now run in it. 
- Not emulated yet: DSP audio, MIDI input, and the eMMC (the SETTINGS menu hangs on the +Drive screen). Turning an encoder doesn't change a value yet.

 Our fixes go upstream to digikit: Digitone support (#19), UI symbols (#21) and pixel-caller tracing (#23).`

[17.09.2026 14:05] bamshanks
Yeah so i have an insert effect on each fx1, which are the stations. Stations just something I call them, because they are multi-fx rather than a single like a stock. 

Then on core 0 on fx 2 I have the delay and on core 1 have the reverb in a hard coded bus setup. 

All the fx2 tracks have a "send" knob to feed into the bus. 

Then on the master I have a character effect with comp and folding and drive and stuff, that has a return knob for feeding in the bus

[17.09.2026 19:14] manobrock
And I thought the OT manual was hard to read. Mother of Christ what
"A modification is a module (modules/<name>/), a selection of modules is a remix (remixes/<name>.py), and make image REMIX=<name> composes a remix into a card-flashable image: placing code, wiring hooks by symbol, refusing collisions by name, and proving every ported module against its author's own build byte for byte. No firmware is distributed here; every image is derived from the user's own 1.40C on the user's machine."


[17.09.2026 19:17] bryant12345
Module is an effect. A remix is a collection of effects. “Make image …” is the command to create the .bin you’d load on your OT to get that collection. 🙂

[18.09.2026 16:45] yldopactyly
Right now, what I'm preliminarily interested in what people could do to map out the possibility space. Octa and others are purely digital. I don't know how much or what is possible with firmware for a digitally-controlled analog device. My instinct tells me it's a little more complicated than getting an ostensibly digital device to behave differently. But I do think lots of interesting stuff, on its face, sounds plausible, like just expanding LFO routing etc., maybe adding keytrack to modulation menu (would be huge).


[18.09.2026 16:46] yldopactyly
As I understand it, only the audio signal path from oscillators to amps in A4 is pure analog - it is otherwise a computer.


[18.09.2026 16:47] yldopactyly
However, AR's machines show that it is the intended proprietary function of the device to have "plugins," software written to run its analog circuits, which is what the machines basically are. They are drop in audio generators which just configure the circuit. What a model could do when given this info and some time to learn about the FW, who knows. New machines?
